L1235: ## Hypercalcemia
L1236: Six subjects experienced a total of 9 'hypercalcemia' or 'blood calcium increased' events. One event resulted in an interruption of study drug in addition to a reduction in study drug dosing. One of the 9 events was considered unresolved at the time of the report. Here are brief narratives of the six subjects:
L1237: - Subject 1002-0021 was reported to have moderate hypercalcemia (lab value not reported) on Study Day 4 lasting for 4 days. At the time, the subject was on no calcitriol and 750 mg calcium daily. The drug regimen was changed to every other day dosing (50 µg QOD) and subsequently decreased to 25 µg daily. On Day 333, the subject experienced an intermittent episode of mild hypercalcemia, which lasted 5 days and resolved on Day 338.
- Subject 1003-0003 had two events of hypercalcemia on Days 28 (10.9 mg/dL) and 223 (10.5 mg/dL). The first episode lasted for 10 days and resulted in a decrease in calcium and calcitriol doses. The second episode lasted for 42 days which resulted in the down titration of study drug from 100 to 75 µg.
- Subject 1006-0007 had a continuous episode of hypocalcemia on Study Day 595 lasting 3 days. At the time the subjects was not taking any oral supplements and the dose of study drug was 100 µg. Study drug was briefly interrupted and then resumed. This event was considered unrelated to study drug.
- Subject 1006-0009 had hypercalcemia (10.5 mg/dL) on Day 2 on a dose of 50 µg. On Day 15, serum calcium was 10.6 mg/dL, the dose of study drug was held, and calcitriol was held for 4 days. Study drug was decreased to 25 µg, supplements were re-introduced and hypercalcemia was resolved.
- Subject 1008-0004 was noted to have hypercalcemia on Day 2 which lasted for approximately 2 months and resulted in a decrease of study drug from 50 to 25 µg. The subject appeared to maintain normocalcemia until Day 344, the subject had another episode of mild hypercalcemia but did not required any change in medication.
- Subject 1020-0006 had hypercalcemia on Day 29 which did not result in medication change. The subject discontinued on Day 107 of his own accord ("too many pills").
L1238: ## Hypocalcemia
L1239: Eighteen subjects experienced 30 events of 'hypocalcemia' or 'blood calcium decreased'. In only 4 subjects did the event occur prior to Week 24. When the event(s) occurred, 8 subjects were on 100 µg, 5 were on 75 µg, 4 on 50 µg, and 1 on 25 µg. Only 2 cases resulted in up-titration of study drug (from 50 µg to 75 µg and from 75 µg to 100 µg). Below are brief narratives for some of these subjects. Only narratives considered notable (SAEs, change in dose, discontinuations) are included here:
L1240: - Subject 1006-1003 had an SAE of hypocalcemia which lasted for 2 days. The calcium level 15 days prior to the event was 7.6 mg/dL. At the time of the event the subjects was on neither oral calcium nor calcitriol. And the dose of study drug was 100 µg. No dose changes were made.
L1241: - Subject 1010-0012 had mild hypocalcemia on Day 7 at which time the subjects was on 50 µg study drug and taking 500 mg/day of oral calcium and no calcitriol. Dose was increased to 75 µg.
- Subject 1014-0005 had moderate hypocalcemia on Day 65 while on a dose of 100 µg. The subject discontinued the trial.
- Subject 1020-0006 discontinued had intermittent hypocalcemia. The subject discontinued on Day 107 due to “subject decision” (“too many pills”).

L1246: ## **SAEs**
L1247: Four subjects had at least one SAE during the trial. The following are brief narratives of these events:
L1248: - This 48 year old woman completed both REPLACE and RELAY. She initiated this trial at a dose of 50 µg, calcium 500 mg and calcitriol 0.25 µg. Eventually, she was up-titrated to 100 µg and the supplements were discontinued. Approximately 50 weeks into the trial, she awoke with **hypocalcemia** symptoms. Her calcium level in the ER was 6.8 mg/dL. She was treated with IV calcium. There was a question of whether there was a relation of her menses to hypocalcemia episodes in the past, since this episode correlated again with the beginning of her menses. This subject had an unrelated SAE of **gastroenteritis** on Day 388.
- This 39 year old woman was being treated with 100 µg study drug when, approximately 75 weeks after initiating the trial, she was admitted with multiple signs and symptoms suggestive of an infection. Her diagnosis was myocarditis from a **viral infection**. This was not considered to be related to study drug.
- This 35 year old woman, who had a history of a syncopal episode, was up-titrated to 100 µg. On Days 36 and 372 after initiating study drug, she had syncopal events. The etiology of the **syncope** was undetermined, but did not appear to be related to abnormal calcium levels.
L1249: - This 41 year old woman had a history of thyroid cancer and cervical cancer and had completed REPLACE and RELAY. Once in this trial, and approximately 131 weeks after initially starting study drug on REPLACE, she was found to have metastatic carcinoma on the lung and underwent a craniotomy of the brain lesions. Study drug was discontinued. She had no history of smoking.

L1283: Figure 1 Scheme for Trial 040 is a timeline diagram illustrating the study phases and treatment groups.
The study is divided into four main phases:
Optimize (~2–16 weeks): Subjects are randomized (Baseline) and receive 50 µg of rhPTH(1-84) or Placebo. Active vitamin D and oral Ca supplements are adjusted during this phase.
Treatment (24 weeks): This phase is further divided into Titration Phase (12 weeks) and Maintenance Phase (12 weeks). During the Titration Phase, subjects receiving rhPTH(1-84) titrate up from 50 µg to 75 µg, and then to 100 µg. Subjects receiving Placebo continue on Placebo. Active vitamin D and oral Ca supplements are adjusted during this phase.
Post Treatment (4 weeks): Subjects continue on their assigned treatment (100 µg rhPTH(1-84) or Placebo) until Week 24 (End of Treatment).
A legend indicates that an upward arrow (▲) signifies "Insufficient response (not achieving independence from active vitamin D and reduced oral Ca to ≤500 mg/day) – titrate up".
L1284: Timeline diagram for the initial 12 months in Trial 008:
Visit 1 Baseline (Week 0) and Last visit in RELAY/REPLACE (Week 4) are marked. The timeline extends to Visits every 8 weeks thru Week 48 (Weeks 16, 24, 32, 40, and 48) and End of Month 12 (Wk 52).
Goal: Total Serum Calcium - 8.0 to 9.0 mg/dL
Initial dose: 50 µg SC (at Visit 1 Baseline).
Adjustment of Calcium/Vitamin D and Titration of NPSP558 are indicated as ongoing processes throughout the trial period.
At Week 4 (Last visit in RELAY/REPLACE), the dose is reduced to 25 µg SC.
Local t. serum calcium (3-5 d) is measured after the dose reduction.
[Local total serum calcium 3 to 5 days after any adjustment of NPSP558 or supplements] is indicated as a monitoring step.
Continue into long-term exter is indicated at the end of the 12-month period (Wk 52).
L1285: Timeline diagram for the long-term extension in Trial 008:
Starts at Week 52 (Continue dose from Week 52).
Visits every 2 months (Months 14, 16, 18, etc.).
Goal: Total Serum Calcium - 8.0 to 9.0 mg/dL.
Adjustment of Calcium/Vitamin D occurs during the treatment phase.
Titration of NPSP558 occurs during the treatment phase.
[Local total serum calcium 3 to 5 days after any adjustment of NPSP558 or supplements] is measured during the treatment phase.
End of Treatment (or Early Termination).
Follow-up (1 week after last dose).
Local t. serum calcium (3-5 d) is measured during the Follow-up phase.
L1286: Flowchart illustrating the Study Design for Trial 007:
Visit 1: Baseline/Randomization.
Local total serum Ca: (3-5 d post-randomization).
Visit 2: (Wk 2) Phone.
Visit 3: (Wk 4).
Visit 4: (Wk 8) End of Treatment.
From Visit 1, subjects proceed to Baseline (up to 2 wk). During Baseline, subjects receive 50 µg SC or 25 µg SC.
Subjects also undergo a "Decrease Calcium/Vitamin D (Local total serum calcium 3-5 d after decrease)" step.
The goal is "Total serum calcium 8.0-9.0 mg/dL".
At Visit 4 (End of Treatment), subjects have the "Option to enter Extension Protocol".
L1287: Flowchart illustrating Subject Disposition—All Subjects:
Screened (N = 184) leads to Screen Failures (N = 60) and Randomized (ITT) (N = 124).
Randomized (ITT) (N = 124) leads to Per Protocol (PP) (N = 106), Safety (N = 124), NPSP558 (N = 84), and Placebo (N = 40).
Per Protocol (PP) (N = 106) is composed of NPSP558 (73) and Placebo (33).
NPSP558 (N = 84) leads to Discontinued Treatment (N = 5) and Completed Study (N = 79). Discontinued Treatment reasons: Subject decision (1), Lost to follow-up (1), Adverse event (2), Other (1).
Placebo (N = 40) leads to Discontinued Treatment (N = 7) and Completed Treatment (N = 33). Discontinued Treatment reasons: Subject decision (3), Investigator decision (3), Other (1). Completed Treatment includes Completed Study (N = 32).
L1288: Flowchart illustrating Subject Disposition for Trial 008—All Subjects:
Completed RELAY (N = 43) and Completed REPLACE (N = 11*) both lead to Eligible (N = 53**).
Eligible (N = 53**) leads to Enrolled (ITT = Safety Population) (N = 53).
L1289: Line graph showing Mean (% Change from Baseline) of Percent Change from Baseline in Calcium Supplementation Dose Based on Investigator-prescribed Data-ITT Population, Excluding Site 1002. The X-axis represents Week (0, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, EOT). The Y-axis represents % Change from Baseline (ranging from -60 to 30). The Placebo group (n=40, dashed line) shows a slight increase, ending around 2.4% at Week 24. The rhPTH(1-84) group (n=84, solid line) shows a significant decrease, starting near 0% and dropping to approximately -50% by Week 12, stabilizing around -50% to -55% from Week 16 to EOT. Error bars are included for all data points.

L1297: Figure 15 is a line graph showing the Mean (\pmSE) of Change from Baseline in 24-Hour Urinary Calcium Excretion (mg/24hr) over time (Weeks 0, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, and EOT) for the ITT Population. The Y-axis ranges from -250.0 to 100.0 mg/24hr. The X-axis shows Week.
Two groups are compared: Placebo (n=40, dashed line) and rhPTH(1-84) (n=84, solid line).
The Placebo group shows a significant decrease in urinary calcium excretion from baseline (0 mg/24hr) to approximately -150 mg/24hr by Week 4, and this decrease is maintained or slightly increases over the subsequent weeks, reaching approximately -170 mg/24hr at EOT.
The rhPTH(1-84) group shows a slight decrease from baseline (0 mg/24hr) to approximately -50 mg/24hr by Week 4, and this decrease is maintained or slightly increases over the subsequent weeks, reaching approximately -70 mg/24hr at EOT.
The Placebo group consistently shows a significantly greater decrease in urinary calcium excretion compared to the rhPTH(1-84) group throughout the study period.

L1337: Scatterplot of Serum Calcium (mg/dL) by Visit for the ITT Population (N=49), excluding Site 1002. The Y-axis represents Serum Calcium (mg/dL) ranging from 6 to 13. The X-axis represents Visit. Horizontal lines indicate the Upper Limit of Normal (10.2 mg/dL) and Lower Limit of Normal (8.6 mg/dL). The data points are labeled "rhPTH(1-84) (N=49)".
Scatterplot of Serum Calcium (mg/dL) by Visit for the ITT Population, Excluding Site 1002. The plot shows serum calcium levels across various visits (B, W4, W8, W16, W24, W32, W40, W48, W52, M14, M16, M18, M20, M22, M24, M26, M28, M30). The Y-axis represents Serum Calcium (mg/dL) ranging from 6 to 13. The X-axis represents Visit. Horizontal lines indicate the Upper Limit of Normal (10.2 mg/dL) and Lower Limit of Normal (8.6 mg/dL). The data points are labeled 'rhPTH(1-84) (N=49)'.
L1338: Scatterplot of Serum Calcium (mg/dL) by Visit—ITT Population, Excluding Site 1002. The plot shows Serum Calcium (mg/dL) on the Y-axis (ranging from 2 to 8) versus Visit (X-axis: B, W4, W8, W16, W24, W32, W40, W48, W52, M14, M16, M18, M20, M22, M24, M26, M28, M30). The data points represent rhPTH(1-84) (N=49). The plot includes a horizontal line indicating the Upper Limit of Normal = 4.5 mg/dL and another line indicating the Lower Limit of Normal = 2.5 mg/dL.
L1339: Scatterplot of 24-hour Urinary Calcium (mg/24h) by Visit—ITT Population, Excluding Site 1002. The plot shows 24-Hour Urine Calcium (mg/24h) on the Y-axis (ranging from 0 to 1000) versus Visit (X-axis: Baseline, Week 16, Week 32, Week 52, Month 16, Month 20, Month 24, Month 28). The data points represent rhPTH(1-84) (N=49). The plot includes a horizontal line indicating the Upper Limit of Normal = 300 mg/24h and another line indicating the Lower Limit of Normal = 50 mg/24h.
